To prevent rafter ends from direct sun and rain, the drip tiles and tile-ends at the eave are both very large, one紧挨着 one, completely covering the rafter ends. Drip tiles have long rectangular shovel shape, 25 cm × 20 cm; oblong shape, 10 cm × 20 cm; ruyi shape; ruyi-shovel shape; and square shape. Drip tiles often have shallow relief patterns, such as fortune-longevity characters, bats, beast heads, water wave patterns, curling grass patterns, evergreen. When rain is abundant, green fuzzy moss grows on the tile surfaces and drip tiles, very ancient-looking.

To protect rafter ends, a very thin square brick is also used, nailed one by one on the rafter ends, closely joined into a neat row, protecting rafter ends from rain. This method is much more effective than using large drip tiles. Wooden boards are also used as rafter-protecting boards; wood easily rots, less effective than brick, but replacing wooden boards is after all more convenient than replacing rafters. For gable roofs, the purlin ends are generally exposed on the gable surface, prone to rotting, so tiles are nailed on the purlin ends for protection. There are rise-head timbers on purlin ends; on the ridge purlin, besides rise-head timbers there are also ridge-supporting timbers, so the tiles nailed on purlin ends are relatively large; on the ridge purlin there are often upper and lower两块, covering the entire exposed purlin ends, rise-head timbers, and ridge-supporting timbers.

本节术语 Key Terms

砖石作 砖墙 石墙 用材 构造 审美价值 乡土特色 公共建筑 较好住宅 后檐墙 山墙 手工制作砖 规格多差异大 28厘米×12.5厘米×3.8厘米 围护作用 不承重 空斗做法 三顺一丁 二顺一丁 一顺一丁 变异形式 节省材料 保温隔热性能 美观 不牢固 实砖墙 毛石墙 条砖平砌双层墙 0.5-0.8米 稳定性牢固性

后记

Postscript
后记
中文原文重构

这次楠溪江中游乡土建筑研究是在1990年10月至1991年12月之间进行的。这期间有调查，有测绘，有摄影，陆陆续续好几次。主持这次研究的是陈志华、楼庆西和李秋香，另外有十一位学生参加了测绘，他们是丘健、张旗、葛志红、何培基、莫军、杨哲怡、潘彤、刘晓梅、张葵、周榕和陈彤。陈志华主要负责研究的整体设计，撰写了本书的前言和第一、二、三篇。楼庆西主要负责摄影。李秋香主要负责测绘，并且撰写了本书的第四篇。研究生舒楠也参加了测绘。建筑学院资料室负责照片的管理和制作。

这次研究的全部经费由台湾龙虎文化基金会支付，由《汉声》杂志社编印出版。下决心放下手边熟悉了的工作，从头做乡土建筑研究，原因之一是急于抢救这些资料。大量珍贵的建筑遗产正在迅速毁灭，永远的失去，不给它们留下一份记录是不可原谅的错误。在研究过程之中，短短一年时间里，楠溪江的乡土建筑就继续破坏了不少。有些是因年久失修而倒塌，如构成坦下村美丽的轮廓的一栋住宅。有些是被拆除，以便在它们的基地上造新房子，如芙蓉村的一栋住宅。有些是遭到意外的灾害，如楠溪江唯一幸存下来的一座书院，被一场大火烧光了明伦堂。有些被改造成现代建筑，如岩头村丽水街上的老店面。更教人心焦如焚的，是有些建筑被热心的"保护"者修缮得面目全非，如苍坡村的东池上造了钢筋混凝土的"九曲桥"，仁济庙屋脊上塑起了两条五颜六色的游龙，张牙舞爪，与朴素淡雅的楠溪江村落格格不入。这本书里的有些测绘图和照片，刚刚完成，对象就已经没有了，或者彻底改变了。因此，保存了这些资料值得庆幸，但资料的不够全面，不够细致，又成了永远的遗憾。虽然楠溪江乡土建筑还有许多课题待做，但这一次的研究不得不结束了。这一次工作的收获之一是更深入、更真切地认识到了乡土建筑的研究非做不可，要快做，赶紧做，迫在眉睫，应该以很大的规模去做。这次工作的另一收获是，认识到了工作的一些难点。例如，乡土建筑大多数不能鉴定年代，不容易弄清村落的规划和建筑的变化过程，有时候是追索原状还是着眼于现状，也举棋难定。本书虽然还是着眼于现状，但章节写了第二章"耕读生活与山水情怀"和第三章"村落规划和建设的工作机制"，以探讨聚落和建筑形成的社会根由以及它们所反映的文化心理，但是，由于缺乏足够的文字资料，农村里又早已没有了了解过去生活的人，这两章写得不很充分。

还有一些通常认为很简单的技术，也成了难题，例如，这些用原木和蛮石建造的房屋，应该怎样制图，本书采用尺规制图，失去了它们粗犷的野性，而如实地画出弯弯曲曲的柱子、梁枋和卵石墙，又几乎是不可能的。采用折中的方法，徒手画直线，恐怕既得不到野性的神韵，又显得草率。以后的每一个课题，怎样捕捉它们各自的特色，大概也会是一个难点。希望在乡土建筑的研究中，每次都有所发现，有所发明，有所创造，有所前进，而不是停留在一个模式上。

1992年春

English Translation

This research on vernacular architecture in the middle Nanxi River was conducted between October 1990 and December 1991. During this period there were investigations, surveys, and photography, conducted several times intermittently. The research was led by Chen Zhihua, Lou Qingxi, and Li Qiuxiang, with eleven additional students participating in the survey: Qiu Jian, Zhang Qi, Ge Zhihong, He Peiji, Mo Jun, Yang Zheyi, Pan Tong, Liu Xiaomei, Zhang Kui, Zhou Rong, and Chen Tong. Chen Zhihua was mainly responsible for the overall design of the research and wrote the preface and Parts One, Two, and Three of this book. Lou Qingxi was mainly responsible for photography. Li Qiuxiang was mainly responsible for surveying and wrote Part Four of this book. Graduate student Shu Nan also participated in the survey. The资料室 of the School of Architecture was responsible for the management and production of photographs.

The entire funding for this research was provided by the Taiwan Longhu Cultural Foundation, and it was compiled and published by Han Sheng Magazine. One of the reasons for deciding to put aside familiar work at hand and start vernacular architecture research from scratch was the urgency to rescue these materials. A large number of precious architectural heritage are being rapidly destroyed, lost forever; not leaving a record for them is an unforgivable mistake. During the research process, in just one year, the vernacular architecture of Nanxi River continued to be damaged considerably. Some collapsed due to long-term neglect, such as a residence that formed the beautiful silhouette of Tanxia Village. Some were demolished to build new houses on their sites, such as a residence in Furong Village. Some suffered accidental disasters, such as the only surviving academy in Nanxi River, whose Minglun Hall was burned to the ground by a great fire. Some were transformed into modern buildings, such as the old shop fronts on Lishui Street in Yantou Village. Even more distressing is that some buildings have been renovated beyond recognition by enthusiastic "protectors," such as the reinforced concrete "Nine-Bend Bridge" built on the East Pond in Cangpo Village, and two colorful roaming dragons molded on the ridge of Renji Temple, baring fangs and brandishing claws, completely out of place with the plain and elegant Nanxi River villages. Some survey drawings and photographs in this book had just been completed when the objects had already disappeared or been completely altered. Therefore, preserving these materials is worth celebrating, but the incompleteness and insufficient detail of the materials have become an eternal regret. Although there are still many topics to be researched in Nanxi River vernacular architecture, this research had to come to an end. One of the gains of this work is a deeper and more genuine recognition that vernacular architecture research must be done, must be done quickly, urgently, and should be conducted on a large scale. Another gain of this work is recognizing some difficulties in the work. For example, most vernacular architecture cannot be dated, making it difficult to clarify the village planning and the process of architectural change; sometimes whether to trace the original state or focus on the current state is also hard to decide. Although this book still focuses on the current state, it includes Chapter Two "Farming-Reading Life and Landscape Sentiments" and Chapter Three "The Working Mechanism of Village Planning and Construction" to explore the social roots of settlement and architectural formation and the cultural psychology they reflect. However, due to the lack of sufficient written materials and the fact that there are no longer people in the countryside who understand past life, these two chapters are not very充分.

There are also some techniques usually considered simple that have become difficult problems. For example, how should these houses built with raw timber and rough stones be drafted? This book uses ruler-and-compass drafting, losing their rough wildness; but truthfully drawing the crooked columns, beams, and pebble walls is almost impossible. Adopting a compromise method of freehand straight lines恐怕 neither captures the charm of wildness nor appears careful. For each future topic, how to capture their respective characteristics will probably also be a difficulty. It is hoped that in vernacular architecture research, each time there will be discoveries, inventions, creations, and progress, rather than remaining stuck in one model.

Spring 1992
